### Question 035: Transplantation: Part 1 Focus 35 (circulating)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the immunological mechanisms underlying acute cellular rejection in solid organ transplantation, specifically the role of T lymphocytes (CD8+ cytotoxic T cells and CD4+ helper T cells) and their recognition of alloantigens presented by MHC molecules on graft cells. (理解实体器官移植中急性细胞性排斥的免疫机制，特别是T淋巴细胞（CD8+细胞毒性T细胞和CD4+辅助T细胞）的作用及其对移植细胞上MHC分子呈递的异体抗原的识别。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor with an A blood type, while the recipient is blood type B. The patient is started on standard immunosuppression including a calcineurin inhibitor (cyclosporine), mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Three weeks post-transplant, he develops fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, and elevated serum creatinine. A renal biopsy reveals diffuse interstitial inflammation with lymphocytic infiltrates, tubular epithelial cell damage, and evidence of endothelialitis in peritubular capillaries. Immunofluorescence staining shows deposition of C4d in peritubular capillaries. Flow cytometry analysis of the biopsy sample shows a predominance of CD8+ T cells infiltrating the tubules and interstitium, along with some CD4+ T cells. The patient's serum creatinine continues to rise despite dose adjustments of immunosuppressive medications.

Which of the following is the MOST likely mechanism directly responsible for the observed graft injury in this patient?

(A) Antibody-mediated rejection (AMR) targeting donor-specific HLA antigens on endothelial cells, leading to complement activation and endothelial damage.
(B) Acute cellular rejection (ACR) mediated primarily by recipient CD4+ T helper cells recognizing donor MHC class II molecules on antigen-presenting cells within the graft.
(C) Acute cellular rejection (ACR) mediated primarily by recipient CD8+ cytotoxic T cells recognizing donor MHC class I molecules on graft parenchymal cells (tubular epithelial cells).
(D) Chronic rejection characterized by fibrosis and vascular changes, typically occurring months to years after transplantation.
(E) Hyperacute rejection mediated by pre-formed recipient antibodies against donor ABO blood group antigens or HLA antigens, causing immediate graft thrombosis.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, ESRD, kidney transplant recipient (B blood type) from deceased donor (A blood type). This ABO mismatch is relevant but not the primary driver of the acute process described.
*   **Immunosuppression:** Standard triple therapy (calcineurin inhibitor, mycophenolate, steroid) is used, suggesting an attempt to prevent rejection.
*   **Timeline:** Symptoms (fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, elevated creatinine) occur 3 weeks post-transplant, characteristic timing for acute rejection.
*   **Clinical Findings:** Signs of graft dysfunction (elevated creatinine, decreased urine output).
*   **Biopsy Findings:**
    *   Diffuse interstitial inflammation with lymphocytic infiltrates: Suggests immune cell involvement.
    *   Tubular epithelial cell damage: Indicates parenchymal injury.
    *   Endothelialitis in peritubular capillaries: Suggests vascular involvement.
    *   C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries: Indicates complement activation, often seen in AMR, but can also be present in ACR, especially if there's overlap or endothelial injury.
    *   Flow cytometry: Predominance of CD8+ T cells infiltrating tubules/interstitium, some CD4+ T cells. This points strongly towards cellular rejection.
*   **Clinical Course:** Worsening renal function despite immunosuppression adjustment suggests rejection is ongoing and potentially severe.
*   **Question Stem:** Asks for the *most likely mechanism* directly responsible for the graft injury.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
*   **Acute Cellular Rejection (ACR):** This is a T cell-mediated response against donor alloantigens (primarily MHC molecules) expressed on the graft.
    *   **Mechanism:** Recipient T cells (both CD4+ and CD8+) recognize donor MHC molecules as foreign.
        *   **CD4+ T cells:** Recognize donor MHC class II molecules, typically presented by donor antigen-presenting cells (APCs) within the graft. They release cytokines (like IFN-γ, TNF-α) that activate macrophages and recruit other inflammatory cells, contributing to inflammation and tissue damage. They can also help activate CD8+ T cells.
        *   **CD8+ T cells:** Recognize donor MHC class I molecules expressed on virtually all nucleated graft cells (including tubular epithelial cells and endothelial cells). Upon recognition, they directly kill these cells via cytotoxic mechanisms (perforin/granzyme release, Fas/FasL interaction), leading to parenchymal damage.
    *   **Pathology:** Characterized by interstitial infiltrates of lymphocytes (predominantly T cells), tubular epithelial cell damage, and sometimes endothelialitis.
    *   **Timing:** Typically occurs within the first few months post-transplant.
    *   **Flow Cytometry:** Shows T cell infiltrates, often with a predominance of CD8+ cells in parenchymal injury.
*   **Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR):** This is a humoral response mediated by antibodies (often donor-specific antibodies, DSAs) against donor HLA antigens, primarily targeting endothelial cells.
    *   **Mechanism:** DSAs bind to donor HLA antigens on endothelial cells, activating complement (classical pathway) and/or recruiting Fc receptor-bearing cells (NK cells, macrophages). This leads to endothelial cell injury, microvascular inflammation (endothelialitis), thrombosis, and ischemia.
    *   **Pathology:** Characterized by endothelialitis, C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries (evidence of complement activation), and microvascular inflammation.
    *   **Flow Cytometry:** May show B cells and plasma cells, but T cells are not the primary infiltrate.
*   **Hyperacute Rejection:** Occurs within minutes to hours due to pre-formed antibodies (e.g., ABO incompatibility, prior sensitization). Causes rapid graft thrombosis and necrosis. Not consistent with the 3-week timeline.
*   **Chronic Rejection:** Occurs months to years post-transplant. Characterized by progressive fibrosis, vascular changes (graft arteriosclerosis), and gradual loss of graft function. Not consistent with the acute presentation.
*   **Analysis of Vignette:** The biopsy shows lymphocytic infiltrates (T cells), tubular epithelial cell damage, and endothelialitis. Flow cytometry confirms a predominance of CD8+ T cells infiltrating the tubules and interstitium. This pattern is classic for acute cellular rejection, where CD8+ T cells directly attack parenchymal cells (tubules) and CD4+ T cells contribute to inflammation. While C4d deposition is present (often associated with AMR), it can also occur in severe ACR due to endothelial damage and inflammation. The predominant CD8+ T cell infiltrate targeting parenchymal cells strongly points to ACR as the primary mechanism.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Antibody-mediated rejection (AMR):** While C4d deposition is present, the *predominant* finding is a CD8+ T cell infiltrate targeting tubular epithelial cells. AMR typically involves DSAs targeting endothelial cells, leading to endothelialitis and microvascular injury as the primary pathology. The flow cytometry showing CD8+ predominance argues against AMR being the *primary* mechanism, although overlap syndromes exist. C4d can be seen in ACR, especially with endothelialitis.
- (B) **Acute cellular rejection (ACR) mediated primarily by CD4+ T cells:** CD4+ T cells play a role in ACR by activating macrophages and helping CD8+ T cells, but the *direct* killing of parenchymal cells (tubular epithelial cells) is primarily mediated by CD8+ cytotoxic T cells recognizing MHC class I. The flow cytometry showing CD8+ predominance makes this option less likely than (C) as the *primary* mechanism of parenchymal injury.
- (D) **Chronic rejection:** This occurs much later (months to years) and involves fibrosis and vascular changes, not the acute lymphocytic infiltrate and tubular damage seen here.
- (E) **Hyperacute rejection:** This occurs immediately (minutes to hours) due to pre-formed antibodies and causes rapid thrombosis. The 3-week timeline rules this out.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Acute Cellular Rejection (ACR) | Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR) | Hyperacute Rejection | Chronic Rejection |
| :---------------------- | :----------------------------- | :-------------------------------- | :------------------- | :---------------- |
| **Timing**              | Days to months                 | Days to months                     | Minutes to hours     | Months to years   |
| **Mediator**            | T cells (CD4+, CD8+)            | Antibodies (DSAs)                  | Pre-formed Abs      | T cells, Fibrosis |
| **Primary Target**      | Parenchymal cells, Endothelium | Endothelial cells                  | Endothelium          | Vessels, Fibrosis |
| **Key Pathology**       | Lymphocytic infiltrates, Tubule damage, Endothelialitis | Endothelialitis, C4d deposition, Microvascular injury | Thrombosis, Necrosis | Fibrosis, Arteriosclerosis |
| **Flow Cytometry**      | T cell predominance (often CD8+) | B cells, Plasma cells              | N/A                 | Variable          |
| **C4d Deposition**      | Possible, especially with endothelialitis | Characteristic                    | N/A                 | Possible          |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Acute cellular rejection is primarily T cell-mediated, with CD8+ T cells directly killing graft parenchymal cells (like renal tubules) and CD4+ T cells contributing to inflammation. The histological hallmark is lymphocytic infiltration, often with CD8+ predominance in parenchymal injury. Antibody-mediated rejection targets endothelial cells via DSAs and complement activation (C4d deposition). Differentiating between ACR and AMR based on biopsy findings (T cell vs. B cell/plasma cell infiltrates, C4d location, parenchymal vs. endothelial injury) is crucial for management.

### Question 036: Transplantation: Part 1 Focus 36 (plasma)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of plasma cells and antibodies in transplant rejection, specifically antibody-mediated rejection (AMR) and the target antigens involved. (理解浆细胞和抗体在移植排斥中的作用，特别是抗体介导的排斥 (AMR) 以及涉及的靶抗原。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor. His pre-transplant panel reactive antibody (PRA) screen was negative. He is started on standard immunosuppression including a calcineurin inhibitor, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Three months post-transplant, he presents with acute graft dysfunction, characterized by rising serum creatinine, proteinuria, and decreased urine output. A renal biopsy reveals evidence of C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries and glomerulitis. Flow cytometry analysis of peripheral blood shows a significant increase in CD138+ plasma cells compared to baseline. Serum analysis reveals the presence of donor-specific antibodies (DSAs) targeting HLA class II antigens.

Which of the following is the most likely mechanism underlying this patient's acute graft dysfunction?

(A) T-cell mediated rejection involving CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes directly attacking graft tubular epithelial cells.
(B) Antibody-mediated rejection mediated by pre-formed DSAs binding to endothelial cells, activating complement, and causing endothelial injury.
(C) Chronic antibody-mediated rejection characterized by transplant glomerulopathy (TG) and fibrosis, typically occurring years after transplantation.
(D) Acute cellular rejection mediated by recipient T cells recognizing donor MHC class I molecules presented on graft cells.
(E) Complement-mediated injury due to activation of the alternative pathway by bacterial products contaminating the graft.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, diabetic nephropathy, kidney transplant recipient. This sets the stage for potential complications post-transplant.
- **Timeline:** 3 months post-transplant. This timeframe is typical for acute rejection episodes, both cellular and antibody-mediated.
- **Presentation:** Acute graft dysfunction (rising creatinine, proteinuria, decreased urine output). This indicates a problem with the transplanted kidney's function.
- **Pre-transplant PRA:** Negative. This suggests no pre-existing DSAs, making *de novo* DSA formation more likely.
- **Immunosuppression:** Standard regimen (CNI, MMF, steroids). Despite immunosuppression, rejection can still occur.
- **Biopsy Findings:** C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries and glomerulitis. C4d is a marker of complement activation, strongly suggesting antibody-mediated injury. Glomerulitis further points towards immune complex deposition or direct antibody attack within the glomeruli.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Increased CD138+ plasma cells. CD138 is a marker for plasma cells, indicating an active humoral immune response, likely producing DSAs.
- **Serology:** Presence of DSAs targeting HLA class II antigens. This confirms the presence of antibodies specifically directed against donor HLA molecules, the primary target in AMR.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the *most likely mechanism*. The combination of acute graft dysfunction, C4d deposition, increased plasma cells, and DSAs strongly points towards antibody-mediated rejection.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR):** AMR is a major cause of graft loss. It involves antibodies (primarily DSAs) binding to antigens expressed on the graft endothelium (HLA class I and II, MICA, etc.).
- **Mechanism:**
    1.  **Antibody Binding:** DSAs bind to endothelial cells lining the graft vasculature.
    2.  **Complement Activation:** Binding of DSAs, especially IgG subclasses, activates the classical complement pathway. C4d deposition is a stable marker of this activation.
    3.  **Endothelial Injury:** Complement activation leads to the formation of the membrane attack complex (MAC), endothelial cell lysis, and release of pro-inflammatory mediators.
    4.  **Inflammation:** Recruitment of inflammatory cells (neutrophils, macrophages) further damages the graft.
    5.  **Thrombosis:** Endothelial injury can lead to microvascular thrombosis, causing ischemia and graft dysfunction.
- **HLA Class II Antigens:** These are major targets for DSAs in AMR. They are expressed on endothelial cells, B cells, macrophages, and dendritic cells. Antibodies against HLA class II are particularly associated with severe AMR.
- **Plasma Cells:** These are terminally differentiated B lymphocytes responsible for producing large quantities of antibodies. An increase in plasma cells indicates an active humoral response generating DSAs.
- **C4d Deposition:** A key histological marker of AMR, indicating recent complement activation in the graft microvasculature.
- **Distinction from Acute Cellular Rejection (ACR):** ACR is primarily mediated by T cells (CD4+ helper T cells and CD8+ cytotoxic T cells) recognizing donor MHC molecules. Histology shows interstitial inflammation with T cell infiltrates, while C4d deposition is typically absent.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): T-cell mediated rejection involving CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes directly attacking graft tubular epithelial cells. This describes a form of acute cellular rejection (ACR), specifically involving direct cytotoxicity. While ACR is common, the presence of C4d deposition and DSAs strongly points away from this mechanism. ACR typically involves T-cell infiltrates in the interstitium and tubules, not C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries.
- (C): Chronic antibody-mediated rejection characterized by transplant glomerulopathy (TG) and fibrosis, typically occurring years after transplantation. While this patient has DSAs and evidence of AMR, the presentation is *acute* (3 months post-transplant) with acute graft dysfunction. Chronic AMR develops over a longer period and is characterized by specific histological features like TG (glomerular basement membrane duplication) and interstitial fibrosis/tubular atrophy, which are not described here.
- (D): Acute cellular rejection mediated by recipient T cells recognizing donor MHC class I molecules presented on graft cells. This describes another form of ACR, often involving CD8+ T cells recognizing MHC class I or CD4+ T cells recognizing MHC class II. Similar to option A, this mechanism does not explain the C4d deposition or the specific findings related to DSAs and plasma cells.
- (E): Complement-mediated injury due to activation of the alternative pathway by bacterial products contaminating the graft. While bacterial contamination can cause graft dysfunction and activate complement, it typically presents with signs of infection (fever, leukocytosis) and would not explain the presence of DSAs, increased plasma cells, or the specific pattern of C4d deposition seen in AMR. Furthermore, the alternative pathway activation usually leads to C3 deposition, not necessarily C4d, and lacks the specificity of DSA-mediated classical pathway activation.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Correct Answer (B) - AMR (Classical Pathway) | Distractor (A) - ACR (Cellular) | Distractor (C) - Chronic AMR | Distractor (D) - ACR (Cellular) | Distractor (E) - Infection/Alt. Pathway |
| :---------------------- | :------------------------------------------- | :----------------------------- | :--------------------------- | :----------------------------- | :-------------------------------------- |
| **Primary Mediator**    | Antibodies (DSAs)                             | T cells                        | Antibodies (DSAs)            | T cells                        | Bacterial products/Alt. Pathway         |
| **Key Finding**         | C4d deposition, DSAs, ↑ Plasma Cells          | T cell infiltrates             | TG, Fibrosis                 | T cell infiltrates             | Signs of infection, C3 deposition       |
| **Complement Pathway**  | Classical (C4d deposition)                    | Minimal/None                   | Classical (C4d deposition)    | Minimal/None                   | Alternative (C3 deposition)             |
| **Timing**              | Acute (days to months)                        | Acute (days to months)          | Chronic (years)              | Acute (days to months)          | Acute/Subacute                          |
| **Target Antigen**      | HLA Class I/II, MICA                          | Donor MHC I/II                 | HLA Class I/II, MICA          | Donor MHC I/II                 | Bacterial products                      |
| **Histology**           | Endothelialitis, Glomerulitis, C4d+ capillaries | Interstitial/Tubular infiltrates | TG, Fibrosis                 | Interstitial/Tubular infiltrates | Inflammation, potential necrosis         |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Antibody-mediated rejection (AMR) is characterized by DSAs targeting graft antigens (especially HLA), leading to complement activation (C4d deposition) and endothelial injury. Key features include the presence of DSAs, increased plasma cells, and C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries. Differentiate AMR from acute cellular rejection (ACR), which is T-cell mediated and lacks C4d deposition, and chronic AMR, which occurs later and has distinct histological features like transplant glomerulopathy.

<a id='question-037'></a>

### Question 037: Transplantation: Part 1 Focus 37 (infused)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the immunological mechanisms underlying graft rejection, specifically the role of T cells (CD4+ and CD8+) and antibodies in recognizing foreign MHC molecules and alloantigens, leading to cell-mediated and antibody-mediated rejection, respectively. (理解移植物排斥的免疫机制，特别是T细胞（CD4+和CD8+）和抗体在识别外源MHC分子和异体抗原中的作用，导致细胞介导性和抗体介导的排斥。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor with an unknown HLA type. The patient is started on standard immunosuppressive therapy consisting of tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Three weeks post-transplant, he develops fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, and elevated serum creatinine. A renal biopsy reveals diffuse interstitial inflammation with eosinophils, neutrophils, and lymphocytes infiltrating the tubules and glomeruli. Immunofluorescence staining shows deposition of C4d in the peritubular capillaries. Flow cytometry analysis of peripheral blood lymphocytes shows a significant increase in CD4+ T cells reactive to donor MHC class II molecules and CD8+ T cells reactive to donor MHC class I molecules. Serum analysis reveals the presence of donor-specific antibodies (DSAs) against HLA-DR and HLA-DQ antigens.

Which of the following immunological mechanisms is MOST likely responsible for this patient's acute kidney graft rejection?

(A) Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) mediated by NK cells recognizing antibody-coated graft endothelial cells.
(B) Complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) mediated by DSAs binding to donor MHC class I molecules on graft endothelial cells.
(C) Direct allorecognition by host CD4+ T cells recognizing intact donor MHC class II molecules on donor antigen-presenting cells (APCs) within the graft.
(D) Indirect allorecognition by host CD8+ T cells recognizing processed donor MHC class I peptides presented by host APCs.
(E) Antibody-mediated complement activation leading to endothelial injury and thrombosis, primarily involving C5a-mediated neutrophil recruitment.

#### Correct Answer: (C)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, end-stage renal disease, kidney transplant recipient. This sets the stage for transplant rejection.
- **Immunosuppression:** Tacrolimus (calcineurin inhibitor), mycophenolate mofetil (purine synthesis inhibitor), prednisone (corticosteroid). Standard therapy, but rejection can still occur.
- **Timeline:** 3 weeks post-transplant. This timeframe is typical for acute rejection, particularly T-cell mediated rejection (TCMR) or antibody-mediated rejection (AMR).
- **Symptoms:** Fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, elevated creatinine. Classic signs of acute graft dysfunction/rejection.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Diffuse interstitial inflammation (lymphocytes, eosinophils, neutrophils), tubular and glomerular infiltration. Suggests both cellular and potentially antibody-mediated injury.
- **Immunofluorescence:** C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries. A hallmark of antibody-mediated rejection (AMR), indicating complement activation.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Increased CD4+ T cells reactive to donor MHC class II, CD8+ T cells reactive to donor MHC class I. Indicates both cellular arms of alloreactivity are present.
- **Serology:** Donor-specific antibodies (DSAs) against HLA-DR and HLA-DQ. Confirms the presence of antibodies targeting donor antigens, strongly suggesting AMR.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the MOST likely immunological mechanism. The presence of DSAs, C4d deposition, and T-cell reactivity points towards a mixed picture, but the question asks for *a* mechanism. The biopsy findings (inflammation) and T-cell reactivity strongly implicate T-cell mediated mechanisms. The presence of DSAs and C4d deposition points to AMR. However, the question asks for the *most likely* mechanism responsible for the *rejection* process itself, considering the cellular infiltrate and T-cell reactivity. Direct allorecognition by T cells is a fundamental mechanism of acute cellular rejection.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Transplant Rejection Overview:** Rejection is an immune response against the transplanted organ (graft). It can be mediated by T cells (cellular rejection) or antibodies (antibody-mediated rejection).
- **T-Cell Mediated Rejection (TCMR):**
    - **Direct Pathway:** Recipient T cells (CD4+ and CD8+) directly recognize intact foreign MHC molecules (allo-MHC) expressed on donor antigen-presenting cells (APCs) within the graft. CD4+ T cells recognize donor MHC class II, and CD8+ T cells recognize donor MHC class I. This requires minimal processing and presentation by host APCs. It's a potent pathway, especially in the early stages.
    - **Indirect Pathway:** Recipient APCs process donor MHC molecules (taken up from the graft) and present peptides derived from these molecules on their own MHC molecules (host MHC class I or class II) to recipient T cells. This is similar to how the immune system responds to foreign pathogens.
- **Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR):**
    - Pre-formed or de novo DSAs bind to donor HLA antigens expressed on graft endothelial cells.
    - This binding can activate complement (classical pathway), leading to endothelial injury, inflammation, and thrombosis (C4d deposition is a marker).
    - DSAs can also bind to Fc receptors on effector cells (e.g., NK cells, macrophages), leading to ADCC.
- **Vignette Analysis:** The patient has DSAs and C4d deposition, indicating AMR. However, the biopsy shows significant cellular infiltration (lymphocytes, eosinophils, neutrophils), and flow cytometry confirms T-cell reactivity to donor MHC. The question asks for the *most likely* mechanism. While AMR is clearly present, the cellular infiltrate and T-cell reactivity are hallmarks of TCMR. The direct pathway of TCMR involves T cells recognizing intact donor MHC molecules, which aligns well with the observed T-cell reactivity and cellular infiltration. The presence of DSAs and C4d indicates AMR is also occurring, potentially contributing to the endothelial damage and inflammation, but the fundamental T-cell recognition of alloantigens is a primary driver of acute rejection, especially in the early phase. Option (C) describes the direct pathway of T-cell mediated rejection, which is a central mechanism in acute rejection.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) mediated by NK cells recognizing antibody-coated graft endothelial cells. This is a mechanism of AMR, but the vignette emphasizes T-cell reactivity and cellular infiltration, making direct T-cell recognition (C) a more central mechanism for the overall rejection process described. While ADCC might contribute, it's not the *most likely* primary mechanism given the strong T-cell evidence.
- (B) Complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) mediated by DSAs binding to donor MHC class I molecules on graft endothelial cells. CDC is a mechanism of AMR where antibodies directly activate complement, leading to cell lysis. While DSAs are present and C4d deposition indicates complement activation, the primary mechanism described in the vignette involves T-cell infiltration and reactivity, suggesting TCMR is a major component. Furthermore, C4d deposition is more indicative of complement activation leading to inflammation and endothelial injury rather than direct cell lysis via CDC.
- (D) Indirect allorecognition by host CD8+ T cells recognizing processed donor MHC class I peptides presented by host APCs. This is a valid pathway for TCMR, but the direct pathway (C) is generally considered more potent and rapid, especially in the early acute phase, as it doesn't require processing and presentation by host APCs. The vignette doesn't provide specific evidence favoring the indirect pathway over the direct pathway. Given the strong T-cell reactivity, direct recognition is a highly plausible and central mechanism.
- (E) Antibody-mediated complement activation leading to endothelial injury and thrombosis, primarily involving C5a-mediated neutrophil recruitment. This describes a key aspect of AMR, where complement activation (indicated by C4d) leads to inflammation and damage. While this is happening (as evidenced by C4d and inflammatory cells), the question asks for the *most likely* mechanism responsible for the rejection. The significant T-cell reactivity and cellular infiltrate point strongly towards TCMR as a major component, making direct T-cell recognition (C) a more encompassing explanation for the overall rejection process described, even if AMR is also present.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Direct Allorecognition (TCMR) | Indirect Allorecognition (TCMR) | ADCC (AMR)                               | CDC (AMR)                                  | Complement Activation (AMR)                 |
| :---------------------- | :---------------------------- | :----------------------------- | :--------------------------------------- | :----------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------ |
| **Primary Mediator**    | T cells (CD4+, CD8+)           | T cells (CD4+, CD8+)            | NK cells, Macrophages                    | Complement system                          | Complement system (C3b, C5a, C5b-9)         |
| **Target Recognition**  | Intact donor MHC on donor APCs | Processed donor MHC peptides on host APCs | Antibody-coated graft endothelial cells | Donor MHC on graft endothelial cells        | Graft endothelium, leukocytes                |
| **MHC Restriction**     | Yes (donor MHC)                | Yes (host MHC)                  | No                                       | Yes (donor MHC)                            | No                                          |
| **Timing**              | Early, potent                  | Later, chronic                  | Variable                                  | Variable                                   | Variable                                    |
| **Vignette Evidence**   | Strong T-cell reactivity       | Less direct evidence           | DSAs present, but less emphasis          | DSAs present, C4d present, but less emphasis | DSAs present, C4d present, inflammation     |
| **Key Mechanism**       | T cell directly binds allo-MHC  | T cell binds allo-peptide/MHC   | Antibody bridges NK cell & target cell   | Antibody activates complement cascade        | Complement cascade activation & downstream effects |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Acute graft rejection involves both T-cell mediated (TCMR) and antibody-mediated (AMR) mechanisms. TCMR occurs via direct (T cells recognize intact donor MHC on donor APCs) or indirect (T cells recognize processed donor MHC peptides on host APCs) pathways. AMR involves DSAs activating complement (leading to C4d deposition, endothelial injury) or mediating ADCC. This case shows evidence of both (DSAs, C4d, T-cell reactivity, cellular infiltrate), but the direct pathway of T-cell recognition is a fundamental mechanism of acute cellular rejection. Differentiate TCMR from AMR based on key findings: cellular infiltrate (TCMR) vs. C4d deposition, DSAs (AMR). Understand the direct vs. indirect pathways of TCMR.

<a id='question-038'></a>

### Question 038: Transplantation: Part 1 Focus 38 (individual)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the immunological mechanisms underlying acute rejection in solid organ transplantation, specifically the role of T cells (CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes and CD4+ helper T cells) and antibodies in recognizing and attacking allogeneic antigens presented by MHC molecules on the graft endothelium, leading to endothelial damage and graft dysfunction. (理解实体器官移植中急性排斥的免疫机制，特别是T细胞（CD8+细胞毒性T淋巴细胞和CD4+辅助T细胞）和抗体在识别和攻击移植排斥抗原的作用，这些抗原由移植物内皮细胞上的MHC分子呈递，导致内皮损伤和移植物功能障碍。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor with an A blood type, while the recipient is blood type B. The patient is started on standard immunosuppression including a calcineurin inhibitor (cyclosporine), an antiproliferative agent (mycophenolate mofetil), and corticosteroids (prednisone). Three weeks post-transplant, he develops fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, and elevated serum creatinine (from a baseline of 1.2 mg/dL to 4.5 mg/dL). A renal biopsy reveals diffuse interstitial edema, infiltration of mononuclear cells (lymphocytes and macrophages) into the tubules and interstitium, and fibrinoid necrosis of peritubular capillaries. Immunofluorescence microscopy shows C4d deposition in the peritubular capillaries. Flow cytometry analysis of peripheral blood lymphocytes shows a significant increase in CD8+ T cells expressing HLA-A2 (donor HLA) and CD4+ T cells expressing HLA-DR (donor HLA).

Which of the following immunological mechanisms is MOST likely responsible for this patient's acute kidney graft dysfunction?

(A) Antibody-mediated rejection (AMR) targeting donor-specific HLA antigens on the graft endothelium, leading to complement activation and endothelial injury.
(B) Chronic rejection characterized by fibrosis and vascular thickening, typically occurring months to years after transplantation.
(C) Acute cellular rejection (ACR) mediated primarily by recipient CD4+ T helper cells recognizing donor MHC class II molecules on graft antigen-presenting cells.
(D) Hyperacute rejection occurring within minutes to hours post-transplant due to pre-formed recipient antibodies against donor ABO blood group antigens or HLA antigens.
(E) Graft-versus-host disease (GVHD) where donor T cells attack recipient tissues, typically seen after hematopoietic stem cell transplantation.

#### Correct Answer: (A)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, ESRD due to diabetic nephropathy, receives kidney transplant. This sets the stage for transplantation immunology.
*   **ABO Incompatibility:** Donor is A, recipient is B. This is a significant risk factor for hyperacute or accelerated acute antibody-mediated rejection (AMR).
*   **Immunosuppression:** Standard triple therapy (calcineurin inhibitor, antiproliferative, steroids) is used, but rejection can still occur.
*   **Timing:** 3 weeks post-transplant. This timeframe is typical for acute rejection (either cellular or antibody-mediated). Hyperacute rejection occurs much earlier (minutes/hours), and chronic rejection occurs much later (months/years).
*   **Clinical Presentation:** Fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, elevated creatinine. These are classic signs of acute graft dysfunction/rejection.
*   **Renal Biopsy Findings:**
    *   Interstitial edema, mononuclear cell infiltration (lymphocytes, macrophages): Suggests inflammation and immune cell involvement, common in both ACR and AMR.
    *   Fibrinoid necrosis of peritubular capillaries: A hallmark feature of AMR, indicating endothelial damage and vascular injury.
    *   C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries: Strong evidence of complement activation, a key mechanism in AMR. C4d is a stable breakdown product of the classical complement pathway C4 convertase (C4b2a).
*   **Flow Cytometry:** Increased CD8+ T cells (expressing donor HLA) and CD4+ T cells (expressing donor HLA) in peripheral blood. This indicates a T-cell response against donor antigens, characteristic of ACR. However, the presence of donor-specific antibodies (DSA) is the defining feature of AMR. The biopsy findings (fibrinoid necrosis, C4d) strongly point towards AMR despite the T-cell findings. The T-cell response can coexist with AMR or even contribute to it, but the specific histological and immunofluorescence findings are most characteristic of AMR.
*   **Question Stem:** Asks for the MOST likely immunological mechanism.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
*   **Transplantation Immunology Overview:** The recipient's immune system recognizes the donor organ as foreign due to differences in MHC (HLA in humans) and minor histocompatibility antigens. This triggers an immune response leading to rejection.
*   **Types of Rejection:**
    *   **Hyperacute Rejection:** Occurs within minutes to hours. Pre-formed recipient antibodies (e.g., anti-ABO, anti-HLA) bind to donor endothelial antigens, activating complement and causing rapid thrombosis and graft necrosis. This patient's ABO incompatibility (A donor, B recipient) puts him at risk, but the timing (3 weeks) rules this out.
    *   **Acute Rejection:** Occurs days to weeks after transplant. Can be cellular (ACR) or antibody-mediated (AMR).
        *   **ACR:** Primarily mediated by T cells. CD4+ T helper cells recognize donor MHC class II on antigen-presenting cells (APCs) in the graft, releasing cytokines (IFN-γ, TNF-α) that activate macrophages and recruit other inflammatory cells. CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) directly recognize donor MHC class I on graft cells, leading to cell lysis. Histology shows interstitial inflammation, tubular injury, and sometimes vascular inflammation.
        *   **AMR:** Mediated by recipient antibodies (donor-specific antibodies, DSAs) against donor HLA or non-HLA antigens on the graft endothelium. Antibody binding activates complement (classical pathway) and recruits inflammatory cells (neutrophils, macrophages) via Fc receptors. This leads to endothelial injury, microvascular inflammation (capillaritis), thrombosis, and graft dysfunction. Histology shows fibrinoid necrosis of arterioles/capillaries, C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries, and endothelialitis. The presence of DSAs is diagnostic.
    *   **Chronic Rejection:** Occurs months to years after transplant. Characterized by progressive fibrosis, vascular thickening (intimal arteritis), and eventual graft failure. Involves both cellular and humoral immunity.
    *   **Graft-versus-Host Disease (GVHD):** Occurs when donor T cells (in the graft) recognize recipient tissues as foreign and attack them. Primarily seen in hematopoietic stem cell transplantation, not solid organ transplantation (where recipient T cells attack donor tissue).
*   **Specific Clues in Vignette:** The timing (3 weeks), clinical signs of acute graft dysfunction, renal biopsy findings (fibrinoid necrosis, C4d deposition), and ABO incompatibility strongly point towards AMR. While T-cell activation is present (flow cytometry), the histological and immunofluorescence findings are pathognomonic for AMR. The ABO incompatibility likely accelerated the development of DSAs or contributed to the severity of the AMR episode.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Antibody-mediated rejection (AMR)**. This is the correct answer. The key features pointing to AMR are the timing (acute, 3 weeks), clinical presentation of acute graft dysfunction, and especially the renal biopsy findings: fibrinoid necrosis of peritubular capillaries and C4d deposition. These are hallmarks of AMR, indicating complement activation and endothelial injury mediated by DSAs. The ABO incompatibility is a risk factor for AMR.
- (B): **Chronic rejection**. This occurs much later (months to years) and is characterized by fibrosis and vascular thickening (intimal arteritis), not acute inflammation, fibrinoid necrosis, or C4d deposition seen in this case. The timing is wrong.
- (C): **Acute cellular rejection (ACR)**. While the flow cytometry shows T-cell activation against donor HLA, the biopsy findings (fibrinoid necrosis, C4d) are not typical for ACR. ACR histology usually shows interstitial inflammation, tubular injury, and sometimes lymphocytic infiltration of vessels, but not prominent fibrinoid necrosis or C4d deposition. ACR is primarily T-cell mediated, while the biopsy strongly suggests antibody and complement involvement.
- (D): **Hyperacute rejection**. This occurs within minutes to hours due to pre-formed antibodies (e.g., ABO). The timing (3 weeks) clearly rules out hyperacute rejection.
- (E): **Graft-versus-host disease (GVHD)**. This occurs when donor immune cells attack the recipient. It is characteristic of hematopoietic stem cell transplantation, where the graft contains immunocompetent T cells. In solid organ transplantation, the recipient's immune system attacks the donor graft, not the other way around.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR) | Acute Cellular Rejection (ACR) | Hyperacute Rejection | Chronic Rejection | GVHD (Hematopoietic SCT) |
| :---------------------- | :-------------------------------- | :----------------------------- | :------------------- | :---------------- | :------------------------ |
| **Timing**              | Days to weeks (can be later)      | Days to weeks                 | Minutes to hours     | Months to years   | Weeks to months           |
| **Mediator**            | Donor-Specific Antibodies (DSAs)  | T cells (CD4+, CD8+)           | Pre-formed Abs      | T cells, Abs      | Donor T cells             |
| **Key Histology**       | Fibrinoid necrosis, C4d deposition | Interstitial inflammation     | Thrombosis, necrosis | Fibrosis, arteritis | Skin rash, GI issues      |
| **Mechanism**           | Complement activation, Endothelial injury | Cytokine release, Cell lysis | Complement activation, Thrombosis | Fibrosis, Inflammation | Donor T cells attack host |
| **Clinical Scenario**   | ABO incompatibility, prior sensitization | Standard transplant          | ABO incompatibility | Late post-transplant | HSCT                     |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Acute rejection in transplantation can be antibody-mediated (AMR) or T-cell mediated (ACR). Key histological features distinguishing AMR include fibrinoid necrosis of capillaries and C4d deposition, indicating complement activation by DSAs. Hyperacute rejection occurs immediately due to pre-formed antibodies. Chronic rejection occurs late and involves fibrosis. GVHD is specific to hematopoietic stem cell transplantation. Differentiating between ACR and AMR is crucial for appropriate treatment.

<a id='question-039'></a>

### Question 039: Transplantation: Part 1 Focus 39 (another.)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the mechanisms of acute rejection in organ transplantation, specifically T-cell mediated rejection involving direct allorecognition of MHC molecules and the role of host T cells recognizing donor MHC on donor cells (Direct Pathway). (理解器官移植中急性排斥反应的机制，特别是T细胞介导的排斥反应，涉及直接识别供体MHC分子以及宿主T细胞识别供体细胞上供体MHC的过程（直接途径）。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor with one HLA mismatch at the HLA-A locus. Post-transplant, he is started on standard immunosuppression with tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Three weeks after transplantation, he develops fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, and elevated serum creatinine. A renal biopsy reveals diffuse interstitial inflammation with lymphocytic infiltrates, tubular epithelial cell damage, and evidence of endothelialitis in peritubular capillaries. Immunofluorescence staining shows C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries. Flow cytometry analysis of the biopsy tissue shows a predominance of CD8+ T cells infiltrating the tubules and interstitium, along with CD4+ T cells. The patient's serum creatinine continues to rise despite dose adjustments of immunosuppressants.

Which of the following mechanisms is MOST likely responsible for the acute rejection observed in this patient?

(A) Antibody-mediated rejection involving pre-formed donor-specific antibodies (DSAs) binding to donor HLA antigens on endothelial cells, leading to complement activation and endothelial injury.
(B) Antibody-mediated rejection involving de novo DSAs binding to donor HLA antigens on endothelial cells, leading to complement activation and endothelial injury.
(C) T-cell mediated rejection via the indirect pathway, where host antigen-presenting cells (APCs) process and present donor MHC peptides via MHC class II molecules to host CD4+ T cells, leading to activation of cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) and helper T cells.
(D) T-cell mediated rejection via the direct pathway, where host T cells directly recognize intact donor MHC class I and class II molecules expressed on donor APCs and other donor cells, leading to activation of CTLs and helper T cells.
(E) Chronic rejection characterized by fibrosis and vascular changes (graft arteriosclerosis) mediated by cytokines like TGF-β and PDGF, typically occurring months to years after transplantation.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, end-stage renal disease, kidney transplant recipient. This sets the stage for transplant rejection.
*   **Immunosuppression:** Standard regimen (tacrolimus, mycophenolate, prednisone) indicates an attempt to prevent rejection, but failure is occurring.
*   **Timeline:** Three weeks post-transplant. This timeframe is characteristic of acute rejection, particularly T-cell mediated rejection (TMR). Antibody-mediated rejection (AMR) can also occur acutely but often involves pre-existing or rapidly developing DSAs. Chronic rejection occurs much later.
*   **Clinical Presentation:** Fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, elevated creatinine. These are classic signs of acute graft dysfunction, suggestive of rejection.
*   **Renal Biopsy Findings:**
    *   Diffuse interstitial inflammation with lymphocytic infiltrates: Hallmark of TMR.
    *   Tubular epithelial cell damage: Common in TMR.
    *   Endothelialitis in peritubular capillaries: Can be seen in both TMR and AMR, but C4d deposition points strongly towards AMR. However, the predominant cell type is CD8+ T cells, which is more typical of TMR.
    *   C4d deposition: Indicates complement activation, a key feature of AMR. This is a crucial clue.
    *   Flow cytometry: Predominance of CD8+ T cells (CTLs) infiltrating tubules/interstitium, along with CD4+ T cells. This strongly suggests T-cell mediated injury.
*   **Rising Creatinine despite Immunosuppression:** Confirms ongoing rejection process.
*   **HLA Mismatch:** One HLA-A mismatch increases the risk of both TMR and AMR.

The question asks for the *most likely* mechanism. The biopsy shows features suggestive of both TMR (lymphocytic infiltrates, CD8+ T cells) and AMR (endothelialitis, C4d deposition). However, the *predominant* cell type infiltrating the graft parenchyma (tubules/interstitium) is CD8+ T cells, which is the effector cell in direct T-cell mediated rejection. The C4d deposition suggests an antibody component, potentially indicating AMR or a mixed rejection picture. Given the strong evidence for T-cell infiltration (especially CD8+ CTLs) directly attacking graft cells, the direct pathway of T-cell mediated rejection is a very strong possibility. The presence of C4d might indicate a concurrent AMR component or that the T-cell response is triggering endothelial damage that activates complement. However, the question asks for the *most likely* mechanism driving the *acute* rejection characterized by lymphocytic infiltration and tubular damage, which is classically associated with T-cell mediated rejection. The direct pathway is the primary mechanism for acute T-cell mediated rejection in the early post-transplant period.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
*   **Transplant Rejection Types:**
    *   **Acute Rejection:** Occurs days to months post-transplant. Primarily mediated by T cells (TMR) or antibodies (AMR).
        *   **T-cell Mediated Rejection (TMR):**
            *   **Direct Pathway:** Host T cells (CD4+ and CD8+) directly recognize intact donor MHC class I and class II molecules on donor APCs (e.g., dendritic cells) and other donor cells within the graft. This requires only one MHC mismatch. CD8+ T cells recognize donor MHC class I on graft cells and kill them directly (CTLs). CD4+ T cells recognize donor MHC class II on donor APCs and become activated, releasing cytokines (IFN-γ, TNF-α) that activate macrophages and CTLs, and also provide help for B cells. This is the predominant mechanism for acute cellular rejection.
            *   **Indirect Pathway:** Host APCs process donor MHC molecules (from damaged graft cells) and present donor MHC-derived peptides via host MHC class II molecules to host CD4+ T cells. This response is similar to a response against any foreign antigen and requires prior sensitization. It is more important in chronic rejection and rejection of vascularized grafts.
        *   **Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR):** Mediated by antibodies (DSAs) directed against donor HLA antigens, particularly on endothelial cells. DSAs can be pre-formed (from previous transplants, pregnancies, or blood transfusions) or de novo (developed post-transplant). Binding of DSAs to endothelial cells activates complement (leading to C4d deposition) and recruits inflammatory cells (neutrophils, macrophages), causing endothelialitis, microvascular injury, and thrombosis.
    *   **Chronic Rejection:** Occurs months to years post-transplant. Characterized by graft fibrosis, vascular changes (graft arteriosclerosis), and progressive loss of graft function. Involves both cellular and humoral immunity, with T cells (Th2, Tregs), B cells, cytokines (TGF-β, PDGF), and complement playing roles.

*   **Key Features in the Vignette:**
    *   **Timing (3 weeks):** Consistent with acute rejection.
    *   **Lymphocytic Infiltrates (CD8+ predominant):** Strong indicator of TMR. CD8+ T cells are the primary effectors in direct TMR, directly killing graft cells.
    *   **Endothelialitis + C4d:** Suggestive of AMR. C4d is a stable breakdown product of complement C4b, deposited at sites of complement activation, particularly in AMR.
    *   **HLA Mismatch:** Increases risk for both TMR and AMR.

*   **Reconciling the Findings:** The vignette presents features of both TMR (CD8+ infiltrate) and AMR (C4d deposition). This is not uncommon; mixed rejection can occur. However, the question asks for the *most likely* mechanism responsible for the *observed* rejection, characterized by lymphocytic infiltration and tubular damage. The direct pathway of TMR is the classic mechanism for acute cellular rejection driven by direct T-cell recognition of donor MHC. While C4d is present, the predominant cellular infiltrate is CD8+ T cells attacking graft parenchyma, fitting the direct pathway. The endothelialitis and C4d could be secondary effects of the intense T-cell response or a concurrent AMR component. Given the classic presentation of acute T-cell mediated rejection with CD8+ infiltration, the direct pathway is the most fitting primary mechanism.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Antibody-mediated rejection involving pre-formed DSAs. While AMR is possible given the C4d deposition, the vignette doesn't explicitly state the presence of pre-formed DSAs (e.g., history of prior transplant, pregnancy, transfusion). Furthermore, the predominant infiltrate is CD8+ T cells, which is less characteristic of pure AMR, where neutrophils and macrophages are often prominent in the microvasculature. The primary pathology described (lymphocytic infiltration, tubular damage) points more strongly towards TMR.
- (B): Antibody-mediated rejection involving de novo DSAs. Similar to (A), AMR is possible, but the strong T-cell component (CD8+ infiltrate) makes TMR a more likely primary driver of the acute rejection picture described. De novo DSAs typically develop over weeks to months, but the acute presentation with prominent T-cell infiltration favors TMR.
- (C): T-cell mediated rejection via the indirect pathway. The indirect pathway involves host APCs presenting donor peptides via host MHC class II to CD4+ T cells. While it contributes to rejection, the *direct* pathway (recognition of intact donor MHC by host T cells) is the primary mechanism for acute cellular rejection, especially in the early post-transplant period with intact graft cells expressing donor MHC. The direct pathway allows for faster and more potent activation of CTLs (CD8+ T cells) against graft cells.
- (E): Chronic rejection. This occurs much later (months to years) and is characterized by fibrosis and vascular changes (graft arteriosclerosis), not the acute lymphocytic infiltration and tubular damage seen three weeks post-transplant.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Direct Pathway TMR (Correct)                                  | Indirect Pathway TMR                                      | Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR)                               | Chronic Rejection                                         |
| :---------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------------ | :-------------------------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------------ | :------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Timing**              | Acute (days to weeks)                                          | Acute, Chronic                                             | Acute, Chronic                                                | Chronic (months to years)                                 |
| **MHC Recognition**     | Host T cells recognize intact donor MHC I & II on donor cells | Host APCs present donor peptides via host MHC II to CD4+ T cells | Antibodies bind to donor HLA antigens (esp. on endothelium) | Complex, involves T cells, B cells, cytokines, fibrosis |
| **Key Cells**           | CD8+ CTLs (direct killing), CD4+ T cells (cytokine help)      | CD4+ T cells                                              | Antibodies, Complement, Neutrophils, Macrophages             | T cells (Th2, Tregs), Fibroblasts, Endothelial cells      |
| **Primary Target**      | Graft Parenchymal Cells (tubules, etc.)                       | Donor APCs (indirectly affecting graft cells)              | Endothelial Cells (microvasculature)                          | Graft Vasculature (arteriosclerosis), Interstitium (fibrosis) |
| **Biopsy Findings**     | Lymphocytic infiltrate (CD8+ > CD4+), tubular damage         | Lymphocytic infiltrate, less prominent than direct pathway | Endothelialitis, C4d deposition, microvascular injury        | Fibrosis, Arteriosclerosis, Tubular atrophy              |
| **HLA Mismatch Req.**   | Yes (usually ≥1 mismatch)                                     | Yes                                                       | Yes (against donor HLA)                                       | Yes                                                      |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Acute T-cell mediated rejection (TMR) is primarily driven by the direct pathway, where host T cells recognize intact donor MHC molecules on donor cells within the graft. This leads to CD8+ CTL-mediated killing of graft cells and CD4+ T cell-mediated cytokine release, causing inflammation and damage. Differentiate TMR from AMR based on biopsy findings (lymphocytic infiltrate vs. endothelialitis/C4d) and predominant cell types (T cells vs. antibodies/complement/neutrophils). Chronic rejection involves fibrosis and vascular changes occurring much later.

<a id='question-040'></a>

### Question 040: Transplantation: Part 1 Focus 40 (previous)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of T-cell receptor (TCR) signaling and costimulatory molecules (CD28-B7) in T-cell activation, specifically in the context of allorecognition during organ transplantation and the mechanism of action of immunosuppressive drugs like cyclosporine. (理解T细胞受体 (TCR) 信号传导和共刺激分子 (CD28-B7) 在 T 细胞活化中的作用，特别是在器官移植过程中发生的全能识别以及环孢素等免疫抑制药物的作用机制。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy undergoes a kidney transplant from a deceased donor. His pre-transplant panel reactive antibody (PRA) score is 10%, indicating low sensitization. Post-transplant, he is started on a standard immunosuppressive regimen including cyclosporine, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Three weeks after transplantation, he develops acute rejection, characterized by fever, flank pain, elevated serum creatinine (from 1.2 mg/dL to 3.5 mg/dL), and biopsy findings showing interstitial edema, lymphocytic infiltration, and endothelialitis. Laboratory tests reveal normal complement levels (C3, C4) and negative donor-specific antibodies (DSA) on crossmatch. The patient's T-cell subset analysis shows a normal CD4+ T-cell count but a significantly reduced CD8+ T-cell count compared to pre-transplant levels. Which of the following mechanisms is MOST likely responsible for the observed T-cell mediated acute rejection despite the immunosuppressive regimen?

(A) Cyclosporine-induced inhibition of calcineurin, preventing IL-2 production and subsequent T-cell proliferation.
(B) Antibody-mediated rejection due to pre-formed or de novo DSA binding to complement receptors on endothelial cells, leading to endothelial injury.
(C) Inhibition of CD28-B7 costimulatory interaction by cyclosporine, preventing full T-cell activation.
(D) Cyclosporine-induced inhibition of the T-cell receptor (TCR) complex assembly, preventing signal transduction.
(E) Cyclosporine-induced inhibition of NF-κB activation, preventing the transcription of genes encoding inflammatory cytokines.

#### Correct Answer: (A)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, end-stage renal disease, kidney transplant recipient. This sets the stage for transplant immunology.
- **Immunosuppression:** Standard regimen (cyclosporine, mycophenolate mofetil, prednisone). This indicates an attempt to prevent rejection.
- **Clinical Presentation:** Acute rejection (fever, flank pain, rising creatinine) 3 weeks post-transplant. This points to an immune response against the graft.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Interstitial edema, lymphocytic infiltration, endothelialitis. These are classic histological features of acute T-cell mediated rejection (TCMR).
- **Laboratory Findings:** Normal complement levels and negative DSA crossmatch rule out hyperacute and significant antibody-mediated rejection (AMR). Reduced CD8+ T-cell count suggests T-cell involvement and potentially some effect of immunosuppression, but rejection is still occurring.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the mechanism responsible for the T-cell mediated acute rejection *despite* the immunosuppressive regimen. This focuses on how cyclosporine works and why rejection might still occur or be incomplete.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **T-cell Activation:** Naive T-cells require two signals for full activation:
    1.  **Signal 1:** TCR binding to peptide-MHC complex on the antigen-presenting cell (APC). This initiates signaling via the CD3 complex associated with the TCR.
    2.  **Signal 2:** Costimulatory signal, primarily CD28 on the T-cell binding to B7 molecules (CD80/CD86) on the APC.
- **Allorecognition:** In transplantation, recipient T-cells recognize donor MHC molecules (alloantigens) as foreign. This triggers Signal 1.
- **Cyclosporine Mechanism:** Cyclosporine is a calcineurin inhibitor. Calcineurin is a phosphatase crucial for activating the transcription factor NFAT (Nuclear Factor of Activated T-cells).
    1.  TCR engagement leads to increased intracellular calcium.
    2.  Calcium activates calcineurin.
    3.  Calcineurin dephosphorylates NFAT, allowing it to translocate to the nucleus.
    4.  NFAT binds to promoter regions of genes, including *IL-2*, driving their transcription.
    5.  IL-2 is a critical T-cell growth factor, promoting T-cell proliferation and differentiation (e.g., into effector and memory cells).
    6.  Cyclosporine binds to cyclophilin, and this complex inhibits calcineurin.
    7.  Inhibition of calcineurin prevents NFAT dephosphorylation and nuclear translocation.
    8.  This blocks the transcription of *IL-2* and other cytokine genes required for T-cell proliferation and effector function.
- **Acute Rejection:** The vignette describes classic features of acute T-cell mediated rejection (TCMR). Despite cyclosporine, rejection occurs, suggesting the drug is not completely effective or that other factors are involved. The key point is *how* cyclosporine works to prevent rejection. It primarily inhibits IL-2 production, which is essential for T-cell clonal expansion and sustained activation.

##### 3. Linking Mechanism to Options
- **(A) Cyclosporine-induced inhibition of calcineurin, preventing IL-2 production and subsequent T-cell proliferation.** This accurately describes the primary mechanism of cyclosporine. By blocking IL-2 production, it prevents the massive clonal expansion of alloreactive T-cells needed for effective rejection. The fact that rejection *still* occurs despite cyclosporine means this mechanism is being targeted, but perhaps not completely suppressed, or the dose is insufficient, or other pathways contribute. This is the most likely mechanism *targeted* by the drug to prevent rejection.
- **(B) Antibody-mediated rejection due to pre-formed or de novo DSA binding to complement receptors on endothelial cells, leading to endothelial injury.** This is ruled out by the negative DSA crossmatch and normal complement levels. AMR typically involves antibodies against donor HLA antigens.
- **(C) Inhibition of CD28-B7 costimulatory interaction by cyclosporine, preventing full T-cell activation.** Costimulation (CD28-B7) is essential for full T-cell activation, but cyclosporine does *not* directly inhibit this interaction. Drugs like CTLA4-Ig (belatacept) target costimulation. Cyclosporine targets the TCR signaling pathway downstream of Signal 1.
- **(D) Cyclosporine-induced inhibition of the T-cell receptor (TCR) complex assembly, preventing signal transduction.** Cyclosporine does not affect TCR assembly. It acts downstream of the TCR signal, inhibiting the calcineurin-NFAT pathway required for IL-2 gene transcription.
- **(E) Cyclosporine-induced inhibition of NF-κB activation, preventing the transcription of genes encoding inflammatory cytokines.** While NF-κB is involved in inflammation and cytokine production, the primary target of cyclosporine is the calcineurin-NFAT pathway, which is crucial for IL-2 transcription, not primarily NF-κB activation in this context.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- **(A):** This is the correct mechanism. Cyclosporine inhibits calcineurin, blocking NFAT activation and subsequent IL-2 transcription, thereby preventing T-cell proliferation, which is essential for rejection. The vignette describes T-cell mediated rejection occurring despite cyclosporine, highlighting the importance of this mechanism.
- **(B):** This describes antibody-mediated rejection (AMR). The vignette explicitly states normal complement levels and negative DSA crossmatch, making AMR unlikely. AMR involves antibodies binding to donor HLA antigens, activating complement, and damaging endothelial cells.
- **(C):** This describes the mechanism of action of drugs like CTLA4-Ig (belatacept), which blocks the CD28-B7 costimulatory pathway. Cyclosporine does not target costimulation; it targets the TCR signaling pathway downstream of Signal 1.
- **(D):** This is incorrect. Cyclosporine does not interfere with the assembly or initial signaling of the TCR complex itself. It acts later in the signaling cascade, specifically inhibiting the calcineurin-NFAT pathway required for IL-2 production.
- **(E):** While cyclosporine can have broader effects, its primary and most well-established mechanism in preventing T-cell rejection is the inhibition of IL-2 production via the calcineurin-NFAT pathway. NF-κB is another transcription factor involved in inflammation, but cyclosporine's main target for preventing T-cell proliferation in rejection is NFAT.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature             | Cyclosporine Mechanism (Correct)                                  | Antibody-Mediated Rejection (Distractor B)                               | CTLA4-Ig Mechanism (Distractor C)                                  |
| :------------------ | :---------------------------------------------------------------- | :---------------------------------------------------------------------- | :---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Target**          | Calcineurin (via cyclophilin)                                     | Donor HLA antigens (via DSA)                                            | CD28 on T-cells                                                    |
| **Pathway Affected** | Blocks NFAT activation -> ↓ IL-2 transcription -> ↓ T-cell proliferation | Antibody binding -> Complement activation / ADCC -> Endothelial damage | Blocks B7 (CD80/86) binding to CD28 -> Inhibits costimulation       |
| **Rejection Type**  | Primarily prevents T-cell mediated rejection (TCMR)                | Causes Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR)                                 | Prevents T-cell mediated rejection (TCMR)                           |
| **Key Lab Finding** | Normal complement, negative DSA (in this case)                     | Positive DSA, often low complement (C4d deposition)                     | Not directly related to complement or DSA                            |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Cyclosporine is a calcineurin inhibitor that prevents T-cell activation by blocking IL-2 production. This is crucial for preventing T-cell mediated acute rejection. Understanding its specific mechanism (targeting calcineurin-NFAT, not costimulation or TCR assembly) is key. Differentiating TCMR (targeted by cyclosporine) from AMR (ruled out by negative DSA/normal complement) is also essential.

<a id='question-041'></a>

### Question 041: Transplantation: Part 1 Focus 41 (chapters,)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of T-cell activation and costimulatory signals (specifically CD28-B7 interaction) in allograft rejection, and how immunosuppressive drugs target these pathways. (理解T细胞活化和共刺激信号（特别是CD28-B7相互作用）在同种异体移植排斥反应中的作用，以及免疫抑制药物如何靶向这些途径。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor. His pre-transplant blood type is A+, and the donor's is O+. He is started on a standard immunosuppressive regimen including a calcineurin inhibitor (cyclosporine), an antiproliferative agent (mycophenolate mofetil), and corticosteroids (prednisone). Three weeks post-transplant, he develops acute onset fever, flank pain, and decreased urine output. Serum creatinine rises from a baseline of 1.2 mg/dL to 4.5 mg/dL. A renal biopsy reveals acute tubular necrosis and interstitial inflammation with lymphocytic infiltrates. Immunofluorescence staining shows C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries. Flow cytometry analysis of peripheral blood lymphocytes shows a decrease in CD4+ T cells compared to pre-transplant levels. The patient's immunosuppression is intensified with antithymocyte globulin (ATG). Which of the following mechanisms is MOST directly inhibited by the standard immunosuppressive regimen (cyclosporine, mycophenolate mofetil, prednisone) to prevent acute rejection in this patient?

(A) Binding of IL-2 to its receptor on T cells.
(B) Activation of the complement cascade leading to C4d deposition.
(C) Interaction between CD28 on T cells and B7 molecules on antigen-presenting cells.
(D) Synthesis of mRNA by inhibiting DNA polymerase.
(E) Production of TNF-α by macrophages.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, ESRD, kidney transplant recipient (A+ -> O+ mismatch, but ABO compatible). This sets the stage for potential allograft rejection.
- **Immunosuppression:** Standard regimen (cyclosporine, mycophenolate mofetil, prednisone). This is crucial as the question asks about the mechanism inhibited by *this* regimen.
- **Clinical Presentation:** 3 weeks post-transplant, fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, rising creatinine. Classic signs of acute rejection.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Acute tubular necrosis, interstitial inflammation with lymphocytic infiltrates, C4d deposition. These findings are characteristic of antibody-mediated rejection (AMR) and T-cell mediated rejection (TCMR), often occurring concurrently in acute rejection episodes. C4d deposition specifically points towards complement activation, often associated with donor-specific antibodies (DSA) in AMR.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Decrease in CD4+ T cells. This is an expected effect of the immunosuppressive drugs, particularly cyclosporine and prednisone.
- **Treatment:** Intensified immunosuppression with ATG. ATG targets T cells, indicating a significant T-cell component to the rejection.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the mechanism *most directly inhibited* by the *standard* regimen (cyclosporine, mycophenolate mofetil, prednisone) to prevent acute rejection.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **T-cell Activation:** T-cell activation requires two signals:
    1.  **Signal 1:** T-cell receptor (TCR) binding to peptide-MHC complex on an antigen-presenting cell (APC).
    2.  **Signal 2 (Costimulation):** Interaction between CD28 on the T cell and B7 molecules (CD80/CD86) on the APC.
- **Mechanism of Standard Immunosuppressants:**
    -   **Cyclosporine (Calcineurin Inhibitor):** Inhibits calcineurin, a phosphatase required for the activation of NFAT (Nuclear Factor of Activated T-cells). NFAT translocates to the nucleus and promotes transcription of IL-2 and other cytokines essential for T-cell proliferation and activation. By inhibiting calcineurin, cyclosporine blocks the production of IL-2 and prevents T-cell activation.
    -   **Mycophenolate Mofetil (Antiproliferative Agent):** Inhibits inosine monophosphate dehydrogenase (IMPDH), an enzyme crucial for the *de novo* synthesis of guanine nucleotides. Lymphocytes (T and B cells) are highly dependent on *de novo* synthesis for proliferation, whereas other cells can utilize salvage pathways. By blocking *de novo* synthesis, mycophenolate inhibits lymphocyte proliferation.
    -   **Prednisone (Corticosteroid):** Has broad anti-inflammatory and immunosuppressive effects. It inhibits the transcription of numerous cytokines (including IL-2, IL-1, TNF-α, IFN-γ) by interfering with transcription factors like NF-κB and AP-1. It also induces apoptosis of lymphocytes and inhibits APC function.
- **Evaluating the Options:**
    -   **(A) Binding of IL-2 to its receptor on T cells:** While IL-2 is crucial for T-cell proliferation, cyclosporine primarily inhibits IL-2 *production*, not its binding to the receptor. Corticosteroids also reduce IL-2 production. This is a downstream effect, but not the *most direct* mechanism targeted by the *entire* standard regimen, especially cyclosporine's primary action.
    -   **(B) Activation of the complement cascade leading to C4d deposition:** C4d deposition indicates complement activation, often associated with antibody-mediated rejection (AMR). While corticosteroids can have some effect on inflammation, the standard regimen (especially cyclosporine and mycophenolate) primarily targets T-cell activation and proliferation, not directly the complement cascade itself. Specific treatments for AMR (like rituximab or plasmapheresis) target B cells or antibodies.
    -   **(C) Interaction between CD28 on T cells and B7 molecules on antigen-presenting cells:** This is the costimulatory signal (Signal 2) required for full T-cell activation. While the standard regimen doesn't *directly* block the CD28-B7 interaction itself (like abatacept does), the inhibition of IL-2 production by cyclosporine and the inhibition of lymphocyte proliferation by mycophenolate mofetil effectively prevent the *consequences* of this interaction, namely T-cell activation, proliferation, and effector function. Furthermore, corticosteroids inhibit APC function, which can indirectly affect B7 expression. Given that T-cell activation is the central event in acute cellular rejection, and the standard regimen's primary targets are T-cell activation and proliferation, this option represents a key pathway affected, even if not blocked at the molecular interaction level by these specific drugs. The question asks what is inhibited by the *regimen* to prevent rejection. The regimen prevents the *outcome* of the CD28-B7 interaction (T cell activation/proliferation).
    -   **(D) Synthesis of mRNA by inhibiting DNA polymerase:** This is the mechanism of action for antiviral drugs like acyclovir or anticancer drugs, not immunosuppressants used for transplant rejection.
    -   **(E) Production of TNF-α by macrophages:** TNF-α is a pro-inflammatory cytokine. Corticosteroids inhibit TNF-α production, but cyclosporine and mycophenolate primarily target T-cell activation and proliferation, not macrophage function directly.

- **Revisiting Option C:** The standard regimen prevents T-cell activation and proliferation, which are downstream events requiring the CD28-B7 costimulatory signal. Cyclosporine blocks IL-2 production (a consequence of T-cell activation initiated by TCR and CD28/B7 signals). Mycophenolate blocks proliferation (a consequence of T-cell activation). Prednisone inhibits cytokine production and APC function. Therefore, the *process* initiated by the CD28-B7 interaction (T-cell activation and subsequent proliferation/cytokine release) is fundamentally inhibited by this regimen. While not a direct molecular blockade of the CD28-B7 interaction itself, preventing the *consequences* of this interaction is the core mechanism by which the regimen prevents T-cell mediated rejection. Among the choices, this represents the most central pathway inhibited to prevent acute rejection.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- **(A) Binding of IL-2 to its receptor on T cells:** Incorrect. Cyclosporine inhibits IL-2 *production*, not its binding. This is a downstream effect, and not the primary mechanism of cyclosporine or mycophenolate.
- **(B) Activation of the complement cascade leading to C4d deposition:** Incorrect. C4d deposition points towards antibody-mediated rejection, which is not the primary target of cyclosporine and mycophenolate. These drugs primarily target T-cell activation.
- **(D) Synthesis of mRNA by inhibiting DNA polymerase:** Incorrect. This mechanism is irrelevant to immunosuppression for transplant rejection. It's characteristic of antiviral or anticancer agents.
- **(E) Production of TNF-α by macrophages:** Incorrect. While corticosteroids inhibit TNF-α production, cyclosporine and mycophenolate primarily target T-cell activation and proliferation. Macrophage activation is not the primary target of this specific regimen.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                     | Correct Answer (C) - CD28-B7 Interaction (Consequences Inhibited) | Option A - IL-2 Binding | Option B - Complement Activation | Option D - DNA Polymerase Inhibition | Option E - TNF-α Production |
| :-------------------------- | :----------------------------------------------------------------- | :--------------------- | :-------------------------------- | :----------------------------------- | :-------------------------- |
| **Primary Target**           | T-cell Activation (Costimulation)                                  | T-cell Proliferation   | Antibody-Mediated Rejection      | Viral/Cancer Cell Replication       | Inflammation                |
| **Mechanism Inhibited**     | T-cell activation, proliferation, cytokine production              | T-cell proliferation   | Complement cascade                | mRNA synthesis                      | Cytokine production         |
| **Drugs Affected**          | Cyclosporine, Mycophenolate, Prednisone (indirectly)               | Cyclosporine, Prednisone | None directly (by this regimen)   | None                                 | Prednisone                  |
| **Relevance to Vignette**   | Central to preventing T-cell mediated rejection                  | Downstream effect       | Points to AMR component            | Irrelevant                          | Secondary effect            |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The standard immunosuppressive regimen for kidney transplant recipients (calcineurin inhibitor + antiproliferative agent + corticosteroids) primarily targets T-cell activation and proliferation to prevent acute cellular rejection. Cyclosporine inhibits IL-2 production (a consequence of T-cell activation), mycophenolate inhibits lymphocyte proliferation, and prednisone broadly suppresses inflammation and cytokine production. The costimulatory signal via CD28-B7 interaction is essential for full T-cell activation, and its consequences (T-cell activation, proliferation, cytokine release) are effectively blocked by this regimen. Key differentials include antibody-mediated rejection (target B cells/antibodies, look for DSA, C4d) and mechanisms of action of specific immunosuppressants (e.g., tacrolimus = cyclosporine, sirolimus = mTOR inhibitor, belatacept = blocks CD28-B7).
